Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)\\r\\n- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`\\r\\n- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)\\r\\n- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`\\r\\n- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`\\r\\n\\r\\nRules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Commands\\r\\n\\r\\nScripts (from `package.json`):\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080\\r\\n- `npm run build` — production build\\r\\n- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags\\r\\n- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle\\r\\n- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)\\r\\n- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)\\r\\n- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode\\r\\n- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`\\r\\n\\r\\nPackage manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.\\r\\n- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\nNo Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Architecture (big picture)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.\\r\\n- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\\r\\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\\r\\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\\r\\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\\r\\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\\r\\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\\r\\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\\r\\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\\r\\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\\r\\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\\r\\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\\r\\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\\r\\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\\r\\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\\r\\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\\r\\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **\\"Joshua\\"**, students display their real profile names.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\\r\\n\\r\\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\\r\\n\\r\\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated \\"it\'s not working\\" / \\"i dont see it\\" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as \\"fixed\\" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). One matrix pass before the push is cheaper than either.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product rules (must not regress)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Foyer (`FoyerSidebar` + `FoyerHeroRing`) is the primary navigation. The Foyer additionally hosts a `GooeyDock` quick-launch row well below the orbital ring; this dock is allowed only on `/foyer`. Do not add docks elsewhere.\\r\\n- Inbox is real messaging with back-and-forth threads and PDF attachments, scoped 1:1 per student with admin.\\r\\n- Admin is fully responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Do not expose actions users cannot actually perform — no fake analytics, no dead buttons, no misleading placeholders. Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake data.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known gaps (do not invent answers)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run them with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows. Nothing runs `typecheck`, `lint`, or the tests automatically — `.claude/safe-push.sh` is the only gate, and only for typecheck.\\r\\n- `README.md` is a stub.\\r\\n- `.claude/settings.json` is set up with a permission allowlist for safe dev work (npm scripts, git reads, MCP GitHub reads) and a denylist for destructive ops. No custom `.claude/agents/`. A `SessionStart` auto-pull hook is wired in `.claude/settings.local.json` (runs `.claude/auto-pull-hook.sh`); Codex mirrors it in `.codex/hooks.json`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skill / agent / command vault\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full skill/agent/command vault lives in the brain root at `~/ai-os/skills/`, `~/ai-os/agents/`, `~/ai-os/commands/` (split into `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees, indexed by `~/ai-os/skills/INDEX.md`). It is inert storage — nothing here auto-loads. To use a vault skill in this project, symlink it into `.claude/skills/` (Codex: `.agents/skills/`). When you create a new skill/agent/command, save it to BOTH this project\'s `.claude/<kind>/` and the vault, so the vault stays the complete catalog.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 9.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'RESULT 1\n# Booking — Context\r\n\r\nThe language of paying for and scheduling tutoring time. Covers the student booking surface (`/schedule`, `/booking`), the public booking microsite (`/book/:slug`), and the Single Session page.\r\n\r\n## Terms\r\n\r\n### Single Session\r\nOne tutoring session paid for individually ($45 for the 70-minute session) rather than out of a Package, booked from a public shareable link by anyone — prospect or current student — without logging in. Needs no Booking Pass. Replaces the Cal.com "LSAT Session" (`session-link`) event, which stays live in parallel until Joshua retires it. _Avoid (per the root glossary): one-off, ad hoc session, hourly._\r\n\r\n### Package\r\nFive or ten hours of tutoring bought upfront through Stripe, consumed by booking sessions out of a Booking Pass.\r\n\r\n### Booking Pass\r\nThe prepaid entitlement a student holds when sessions are **owed** to them, carrying the count owed and the count used, reachable at its own private high-entropy booking link. Granted by Joshua or by a matched package payment. A Single Session never creates or consumes one. _Avoid: booking link (that is the pass\'s URL, not the thing), wallet, credits-as-product._\r\n\r\n### Booker\r\nThe person on the public page picking a slot. Not necessarily an authenticated user or an existing student; identified by the name and email they enter.\r\n\r\n### Slot\r\nA concrete start time offered on the calendar. For the Single Session, slots run at a 70-minute pitch from the start of each day\'s availability window, within the booking window, respecting minimum notice and calendar conflicts.\r\n\r\n### Hold\r\nA short-lived claim on a Slot created when a Booker submits their details and is sent to payment. While live it blocks the Slot for everyone else; it converts to a booking when payment succeeds and evaporates if payment doesn\'t arrive in time.\r\n\r\n### Buffer\r\nThe gap Joshua requires between the end of one calendar event and the start of the next: **10 minutes**, counted once between any two events regardless of which of them was booked where. A Slot is offered only if the whole session plus the Buffer fits clear of everything else, and every check that decides whether a Slot is free uses this same number. _Avoid: padding, breathing room, "15 minutes" (the old number)._\r\n\r\n### Booking Window\r\nHow far into the future slots are offered. The Single Session mirrors Cal.com: 6 business days out, non-rolling.\r\n\r\n### Minimum Notice\r\nHow close to now a Slot may start. Either way\\r\\n    // it is only a bucket key for counting hits, and a missing secret must not\\r\\n    // cost every student the ability to book.\\r\\n    const hmacKey = Deno.env.get(\\"BOOKING_IP_HMAC_KEY\\");\\r\\n    if (!hmacKey) console.error(\\"BOOKING_IP_HMAC_KEY is not set; throttle keys are unkeyed digests\\");\\r\\n    const ipHash = hmacKey ? await hmacHex(hmacKey, ip) : await sha256Hex(ip);\\r\\n    const isWrite = [\\"hold\\", \\"abandon\\", \\"cancel\\"].includes(action);\\r\\n    if (!await rateLimit(supabase, ipHash, isWrite ? action : \\"read\\", isWrite ? WRITE_LIMIT : READ_LIMIT)) {\\r\\n      return json({ error: \\"rate_limited\\" }, 429);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (action === \\"info\\") {\\r\\n      const event = await loadEvent(supabase, body.event);\\r\\n      return json({\\r\\n        host: { name: \\"Joshua\\", title: event.label },\\r\\n        event: event.slug,\\r\\n        duration_minutes: event.lengthMinutes,\\r\\n        amount_cents: event.priceCents,\\r\\n        currency: event.currency,\\r\\n        requires_payment: event.requiresPayment,\\r\\n        timezone: SINGLE_SESSION.hostTimezone,\\r\\n        single_use: event.singleUse,\\r\\n        // A one-off link that has been used still resolves -- the page needs to\\r\\n        // say so rather than show an empty calendar. `note` is never included:\\r\\n        // it is Joshua\'s reminder of who the link was for.\\r\\n        available: !(await linkSpent(supabase, event)),\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (action === \\"slots\\") {\\r\\n      const event = await loadEvent(supabase, body.event);\\r\\n      // A spent one-off link sells nothing. Checked before the cache, because\\r\\n      // whether a link is used changes on a booking, not on a 15-second timer.\\r\\n      if (await linkSpent(supabase, event)) return json({ slots: [], timezone: SINGLE_SESSION.hostTimezone });\\r\\n      // Keyed by event: a consultation and a session have different lengths,\\r\\n      // buffers and windows, so one cached answer cannot serve both.\\r\\n      const cached = slotsCache.get(event.slug);\\r\\n      if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) return json(cached.payload);\\r\\n      const nowMs = Date.now();\\r\\n      const rangeStart = new Date(nowMs + event.minimumNoticeMinutes * 60_000).toISOString();\\r\\n      const rangeEnd = new Date(nowMs + 16 * 86_400_000).toISOString();\\r\\n      const busy = await loadBusyIntervals({ supabase, gcal, rangeStartIso: rangeStart, rangeEndIso: rangeEnd });\\r\\n      const { data: availability, error } = await supabase.rpc(\\"get_booking_availability\\");\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n      const { hostTz, rangesByDow } = parseAvailability(availability);\\r\\n      const payload = {\\r\\n        slots: generateSingleSessionSlots({ nowMs, hostTz, rangesByDow, busy, event }),\\r\\n        timezone: hostTz,\\r\\n      };\\r\\n      slotsCache.set(event.slug, { expiresAt: Date.now() + 15_000, payload });\\r\\n      return json(payload);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (action === \\"status\\") {\\r\\n      const checkoutSessionId = String(body.checkout_session_id || \\"\\");\\r\\n      const token = String(body.booking_token || \\"\\");\\r\\n      if (!checkoutSessionId || !token) return json({ error: \\"invalid_token\\" }, 403);\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc(\\"get_single_session_status\\", {\\r\\n        _stripe_checkout_session_id: checkoutSessionId,\\r\\n        _booking_token_hash: await sha256Hex(token),\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      if (error || !data) return json({ error: \\"invalid_token\\" }, error?.code === \\"P0403\\" ? 403 : 404);\\r\\n      return json(data);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (action === \\"hold\\") {\\r\\n      const name = String(body.name || \\"\\").trim().replace(/\\\\s+/g, \\" \\").slice(0, 120);\\r\\n      const email = normalizeEmail(body.email);\\r\\n      const notes = String(body.notes || \\"\\").trim().slice(0, 2000);\\r\\n      const guests = normalizeGuests(body.guests, email);\\r\\n      const clientIdempotencyKey = String(body.client_idempotency_key || \\"\\").trim();\\r\\n      const startAt = new Date(String(body.start_at || \\"\\"));\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

If not set to this value, the endpoint will default to an older version.\n\n\u200b\n\nAuthorization\n\nstring\n\nvalue must be `Bearer <token>` where `<token>` is api key prefixed with cal_, managed user access token, or OAuth access token\n\n\u200b\n\nx-cal-secret-key\n\nstring\n\nFor platform customers - OAuth client secret key\n\n\u200b\n\nx-cal-client-id\n\nstring\n\nFor platform customers - OAuth client ID\n\n#### Query Parameters\n\n\u200b\n\nusername\n\nstring\n\nThe username of the user to get event types for. If only username provided will get all event types.\n\n\u200b\n\neventSlug\n\nstring\n\nSlug of event type to return. Notably, if eventSlug is provided then username must be provided too, because multiple users can have event with same slug.\n\n\u200b\n\nusernames\n\nstring\n\nGet dynamic event type for multiple usernames separated by comma. e.g `usernames=alice,bob`\n\n\u200b\n\norgSlug\n\nstring\n\nslug of the user\'s organization if he is in one, orgId is not required if using this parameter\n\n\u200b\n\norgId\n\nnumber\n\nID of the organization of the user you want the get the event-types of, orgSlug is not needed when using this parameter\n\n\u200b\n\nsortCreatedAt\n\nenum<string>\n\nSort event types by creation date. When not provided, no explicit ordering is applied.\n\nAvailable options:\n\n`asc`,\n\n`desc`\n\n#### Response\n\n200 - application/json\n\n\u200b\n\nstatus\n\nenum<string>\n\nrequired\n\nAvailable options:\n\n`success`,\n\n`error`\n\nExample:\n\n`"success"`\n\n\u200b\n\ndata\n\nobject[]\n\nrequired\n\nShow child attributes--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nGet available time slots for an event type - Cal.com Docs (https://cal.com/docs/api-reference/v2/slots/get-available-time-slots-for-an-event-type)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn2search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 4 months ago; Get available time slots for an event type ...       --url https://api.cal.com/v2/slots \\ ... eventTypeId=10&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06&timeZone=Europe/Rome’\n\nGet available time slots for an event type\n\ncURL\n    \n    `curl --request GET \\\n      --url https://api.cal.com/v2/slots \\\n      --header \'Authorization: [REDACTED] <token>\' \\\n      --header \'cal-api-version: <cal-api-version>\'`\n\n200\n    \n    `{\n      "status": "success",\n      "data": {\n        "2050-09-05": [\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-05T09:00:00.000+02:00"\n          },\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-05T10:00:00.000+02:00"\n          }\n        ],\n        "2050-09-06": [\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-06T09:00:00.000+02:00"\n          },\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-06T10:00:00.000+02:00"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    }`\n\nSlots\n\n# Get available time slots for an event type\n\nThere are 4 ways to get available slots for event type of an individual user:\n\n  1. By event type id. Example ‘/v2/slots?eventTypeId=10&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06&timeZone=Europe/Rome’\n\n  2. By event type slug + username. Example ‘/v2/slots?eventTypeSlug=intro&username=bob&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06’\n\n  3. By event type slug + username + organization slug when searching within an organization. Example ‘/v2/slots?organizationSlug=org-slug&eventTypeSlug=intro&username=bob&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06’\n\n  4. By usernames only (used for dynamic event type - there is no specific event but you want to know when 2 or more people are available). Example ‘/v2/slots?usernames=alice,bob&username=bob&organizationSlug=org-slug&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06’. As you see you also need to provide the slug of the organization to which each user in the ‘usernames’ array belongs.\n\nAnd 3 ways to get available slots for team event type:\n\n  1. By team event type id. Example ‘/v2/slots?eventTypeId=10&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06&timeZone=Europe/Rome’. Note for managed event types: Managed event types are templates that create individual child event types for each team member. You cannot fetch slots for the parent managed event type directly. Instead, you must:\n\n     * Find the child event type IDs (the ones assigned to specific users)\n     * Use those child event type IDs to fetch slots as individual user event types using as described in the individual user section above.\n  2. By team event type slug + team slug. Example ‘/v2/slots?eventTypeSlug=intro&teamSlug=team-slug&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06’\n\n  3. By team event type slug + team slug + organization slug when searching within an organization. Example ‘/v2/slots?organizationSlug=org-slug&eventTypeSlug=intro&teamSlug=team-slug&start=2050-09-05&end=2050-09-06’\n\nAll of them require “start” and “end” query parameters which define the time range for which available slots should be checked. Optional parameters are:\n\n  * timeZone: Time zone in which the available slots should be returned. Defaults to UTC.\n  * duration: Only use for event types that allow multiple durations or for dynamic event types. If not passed for multiple duration event types defaults to default duration. For dynamic event types defaults to 30 aka each returned slot is 30 minutes long. So duration=60 means that returned slots will be each 60 minutes long.\n  * format: Format of the slots. By default return is an object where each key is date and value is array of slots as string. If you want to get start and end of each slot use “range” as value.\n  * bookingUidToReschedule: When rescheduling an existing booking, provide the booking’s unique identifier to exclude its time slot from busy time calculations. This ensures the original booking time appears as available for rescheduling.\n\nPlease make sure to pass in the cal-api-version header value as mentioned in the Headers section. Not passing the correct value will default to an older version of this endpoint.\n\nGET\n\n/\n\nv2\n\n/\n\nslots\n\nTry it\n\nGet available time slots for an event type\n\ncURL\n    \n    `curl --request GET \\\n      --url https://api.cal.com/v2/slots \\\n      --header \'Authorization: [REDACTED] <token>\' \\\n      --header \'cal-api-version: <cal-api-version>\'`\n\n200\n    \n    `{\n      "status": "success",\n      "data": {\n        "2050-09-05": [\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-05T09:00:00.000+02:00"\n          },\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-05T10:00:00.000+02:00"\n          }\n        ],\n        "2050-09-06": [\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-06T09:00:00.000+02:00"\n          },\n          {\n            "start": "2050-09-06T10:00:00.000+02:00"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    }`\n\n#### Authorizations\n\n\u200b\n\nAuthorization\n\nstring\n\nheader\n\nrequired\n\nBearer authentication header of the form `Bearer <token>`, where `<token>` is your auth token.\n\n#### Headers\n\n\u200b\n\ncal-api-version\n\nstring\n\ndefault:2024-09-04\n\nrequired\n\nMust be set to 2024-09-04. If not set to this value, the endpoint will default to an older version.\n\n#### Query Parameters\n\n\u200b\n\nbookingUidToReschedule\n\nstring\n\nThe unique identifier of the booking being rescheduled. When provided will ensure that the original booking time appears within the returned available slots when rescheduling.\n\nExample:\n\n`"abc123def456"`\n\n\u200b\n\nstart\n\nstring\n\nrequired\n    \n    `  Time starting from which available slots should be checked.\n    \n      Must be in UTC timezone as ISO 8601 datestring.\n    \n      You can pass date without hours which defaults to start of day or specify hours:\n      2024-08-13 (will have hours 00:00:00 aka at very beginning of the date) or you can specify hours manually like 2024-08-13T09:00:00Z.\nIf slug is provided then username or teamSlug must be provided too and if relevant organizationSlug too.\n\nExample:\n\n`"event-type-slug"`\n\n\u200b\n\neventTypeId\n\nnumber\n\nThe ID of the event type for which available slots should be checked.\n\nExample:\n\n`"100"`\n\n\u200b\n\nusernames\n\nstring\n\nThe usernames for which available slots should be checked separated by a comma.\n    \n    `Checking slots by usernames is used mainly for dynamic events where there is no specific event but we just want to know when 2 or more people are available.\n    \n    Must contain at least 2 usernames.\n    `\n\nExample:\n\n`"alice,bob"`\n\n\u200b\n\nformat\n\nstring\n\nFormat of slot times in response. Use \'range\' to get start and end times. Use \'time\' or omit this query parameter to get only start time.\n\nExample:\n\n`"range"`\n\n\u200b\n\nduration\n\nnumber\n\nIf event type has multiple possible durations then you can specify the desired duration here. Also, if you are fetching slots for a dynamic event then you can specify the duration her which defaults to 30, meaning that returned slots will be each 30 minutes long.\n\nExample:\n\n`"60"`\n\n\u200b\n\ntimeZone\n\nstring\n\nTime zone in which the available slots should be returned. ## assistant

I compared the two live calendars for **2026-09-15 through 2026-09-21**, in **America/Toronto**. Both currently show 16 slots across the same six days, with none on September 20. But only **6 start times match exactly**.

| Date | Cal.com link | LSAT Prep link |
|---|---|---|
| Tue, Sep 15 | 1:00, 2:10, 3:20 PM | 1:00, 2:20, 3:40 PM |
| Wed, Sep 16 | 1:00, 2:10, 3:20 PM | 1:00, 2:20, 3:40 PM |
| Thu, Sep 17 | 1:00, 2:10, 4:50 PM | 1:00, 2:20, 3:40 PM |
| Fri, Sep 18 | 1:00, 4:10, 5:20 PM | 1:00, 5:00, 6:20 PM |
| Sat, Sep 19 | 8:00 PM | 8:00 PM |
| Sun, Sep 20 | No slots | No slots |
| Mon, Sep 21 | 2:00, 3:10, 4:20 PM | 2:00, 3:20, 4:40 PM |

Both sessions are 70 minutes and have 24-hour notice. The key difference is that the LSAT Prep calendar adds a 10-minute buffer between sessions; Cal.com has no buffer. That shifts many start times. Cal.com also has a 45-day rolling window, while LSAT Prep offers a shorter six-business-day window.

So the calendars are **not interchangeable**, even though they currently show the same number of openings. I made no changes. I checked Cal.com’s live availability using its [available-slots API](https://cal.com/docs/api-reference/v2/slots/get-available-time-slots-for-an-event-type).

- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. ### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: lsat-u-platform-actions\ndescription: Perform any LSAT U platform action for Joshua directly from the brain workspace — assign guided drills, homework, or activities; post classroom items and notes; send inbox messages; ingest session notes — by driving the deployed app (lsatprep.study) through his logged-in Chrome admin session, verifying the result, and logging it to the student\'s folder. Trigger on "assign <student> …", "post … to <student>", "message <student>", "send <student> homework", "give <student> an activity", or any request to make something happen on the platform from here.\n---\n\n# LSAT U Platform Actions\n\nJoshua\'s workplace is this brain workspace, not the app\'s admin UI. This skill makes\nClaude the operator: he says what should happen to a student on LSAT U, Claude drives\nthe deployed platform to make it happen, verifies it landed, and records it.\n\n## Mechanism\n\nUse the **claude-in-chrome** browser tools against `https://lsatprep.study` with\nJoshua\'s logged-in Chrome admin session. There is no agent path to the prod Supabase\nbackend (Lovable-managed, not in MCP scope) — the browser + admin JWT IS the API.\nDo not insert rows backend-style; act through the same UI flows Joshua would use,\nunless he explicitly asks for a backend repair.\n\n**React controlled-input rule (hard-won 2026-07-16):** `form_input` sets DOM values\nbut often does NOT update React state, leaving submit buttons silently disabled and\nproducing zero network traffic. For every form:\n\n1. Click the field and type with real keystrokes (`computer` type action); for\n   `<select>`s, click to focus then choose with keyboard (first-letter + Enter) or a\n   real option click.\n2. Before clicking submit, confirm the button is actually enabled (full-opacity, or\n   check `disabled` via read_page).\n3. After clicking, confirm via `read_network_requests` that the mutation actually\n   fired (POST to `…supabase.co/functions/v1/…` or `/rest/v1/…`) and returned 2xx.\n   A UI that merely looks done is not evidence.\n\n## Routing — where each action lives\n\nAll under `/teacher` (Teacher Desk). Workspaces via `?ws=`: `people` / `students`,\n`library`, `messages`/`inbox`, `admin`, `gmail`, `drive`.\n\n| Action | Surface |\n|---|---|\n| Assign a guided drill | `/teacher?ws=library&tab=guided-drills` → drill card → Assign. **Delegate to the `guided-drill-assignment` skill** for selection/QA/logging — it is the specialist; this skill is the router. |\n| Assign homework / quiz | Teacher Desk library or the student\'s detail page (`TeacherStudentDetail`) → assign flow → creates classroom item + notification |\n| Assign an activity (e.g. Negation Station) | Library activities surface → Assign; verify resume + notification (see `wiki/tasks.md` activity-centre notes) |\n| Post a classroom item / note | Student detail page → classroom/dossier tabs |\n| Message a student | `ws=messages` (admin side of the 1:1 inbox); supports PDF attachments |\n| Ingest a session transcript | `/teacher/session-ingest` (Phase 1A form: Doc URL + student + optional Gmail message id) or the scan/resolve queue on the same page |\n| Review/publish a session bundle | Student detail → dossier/review tabs (`SessionBundleReview`); publish is approval-gated |\n\nIf a flow\'s exact clicks are unknown, discover them live with read_page/find on the\nreal surface — do not guess selectors from memory, and do not invent UI that isn\'t\nthere.\n\n## Order of operations (every action)\n\n1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one\n   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and\n   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.\n2. **Do the action** through the UI per the routing table, honoring the React rule.\n3. **Verify it landed**: the mutation request returned 2xx AND the UI reflects it\n   (item appears in the student\'s classroom list / message thread / queue). For\n   assignments, verify the notification surface where checkable.\n4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with\n   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to\n   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see\n  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua\'s voice —\n  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\n- If Joshua\'s request fully specifies the action ("assign Lydia the conditional\n  drill"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he\n  hasn\'t dictated), draft it and show him before sending.\n- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn\'t\n  confirmed. Never report an action complete on the strength of a click alone.\n- No student-facing action as a side effect of a different request.\n\r\n# AGENTS.md\r\n\r\nThis file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\n## Project Map\r\n\r\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\r\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\r\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\r\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\r\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\r\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\r\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\r\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\r\n\r\n## Stack And Commands\r\n\r\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\r\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\r\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\r\n- Build: `npm run build`\r\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\r\n- Test: `npm run test`\r\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\r\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\r\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\n## Working Rules\r\n\r\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\r\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\r\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\r\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\r\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\r\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\r\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\r\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\r\n\r\n## Product Constraints\r\n\r\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\r\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\r\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\r\n\r\n## Smart Drill Note\r\n\r\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\r\n\r\n## Workflow Expectations\r\n\r\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\r\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\r\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\r\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\r\n\r\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\r\n\r\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\r\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\r\n\r\n# CLAUDE.md\r\n\r\nThis file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.\r\n\r\n## Project snapshot\r\n\r\nLSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\r\n\r\n- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\r\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\r\n\r\nWhere LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):\r\n\r\nThe vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:\r\n\r\n- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`\r\n- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`\r\n- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`\r\n- A student\'s whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule\r\n- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)\r\n\r\n**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Formatting is ESLint-only.\r\n\r\n## Architecture (big picture)\r\n\r\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\r\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\r\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\r\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\r\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\r\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\r\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\r\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\r\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\r\n\r\n## Conventions\r\n\r\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\r\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\r\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\r\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\r\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\r\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\r\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\r\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **"Joshua"**, students display their real profile names.\r\n\r\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\r\n\r\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\r\n\r\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\r\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\r\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated "it\'s not working" / "i dont see it" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n\r\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as "fixed" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). One matrix pass before the push is cheaper than either.\r\n\r\n## Product rules (must not regress)\r\n\r\n- Foyer (`FoyerSidebar` + `FoyerHeroRing`) is the primary navigation. The Foyer additionally hosts a `GooeyDock` quick-launch row well below the orbital ring; this dock is allowed only on `/foyer`. Do not add docks elsewhere.\r\n- Inbox is real messaging with back-and-forth threads and PDF attachments, scoped 1:1 per student with admin.\r\n- Admin is fully responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Do not expose actions users cannot actually perform — no fake analytics, no dead buttons, no misleading placeholders. Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake data.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email.\r\n\r\n## Known gaps (do not invent answers)\r\n\r\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run them with `npm run test`. ", "isO": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "Does watching someone else eat fill one\'s own stomach?", "isC": true}], "feedback": "Correct. A rhetorical question acting as a rebuttal conclusion."}, {"id": 13, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "Behind the hope that computers can replace teachers is the idea that the student\'s understanding of the subject being taught consists in knowing facts and rules, the job of a teacher being to make the facts and rules explicit and convey them to the student, either by practice drills or by coaching. If that were indeed the way the mind works, the teacher could transfer facts and rules to the computer, which would replace the teacher as drillmaster and coach. ", "isO": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Pivot\\">But</i-w> <i-w t=\\"Premise Indicator\\">since</i-w> understanding does not consist merely of knowing facts and rules, but of the grasp of the general concepts underlying them, ", "isS": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "the hope that the computer will eventually replace the teacher is fundamentally misguided.", "isC": true}], "feedback": "Correct. The conclusion is at the end of the paragraph, rebutting the hope mentioned earlier."}, {"id": 14, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "So-called environmentalists have argued that the proposed Golden Lake Development would interfere with bird-migration patterns. ", "isO": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Pivot\\">However</i-w>, the fact that these same people have raised environmental objections to virtually every development proposal brought before the council in recent years indicates that their expressed concern for bird migration patterns is nothing but a mask for their antidevelopment, antiprogress agenda. ", "isS": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "Their claim, <i-w t=\\"Conclusion Indicator\\">therefore</i-w>, <i-w t=\\"Opinion Indicator\\">should</i-w> be dismissed without further consideration.", "isC": true}], "feedback": "Correct. Rebuttal conclusion marked by \'therefore\' at the end."}, {"id": 15, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "That long-term cigarette smoking can lead to health problems including cancer and lung disease is a scientifically well-established fact. ", "isN": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "Contrary to what many people seem to believe, <i-w t=\\"Pivot\\">however</i-w>, it is not necessary to deny this fact in order to reject the view that tobacco companies should be held either morally or legally responsible for the poor health of smokers. ", "isC": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Premise Indicator\\">After all</i-w>, excessive consumption of candy undeniably leads to such health problems as tooth decay, <i-w t=\\"Pivot\\">but</i-w> no one seriously believes that candy eaters who get cavities should be able to sue candy manufacturers.", "isS": true}], "feedback": "Correct. Rebuttal conclusion nested in a complex sentence, supported by the candy analogy."}, {"id": 16, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "Pharmacists maintain that doctors should not be permitted to sell the medicine that they prescribe <i-w t=\\"Premise Indicator\\">because</i-w> doctors would then be tempted to prescribe unnecessary medicines in order to earn extra income. ", "isO": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Pivot\\">But</i-w> pharmacists have a financial interest in having a monopoly on the sale of prescription medicines, ", "isS": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Conclusion Indicator\\">so</i-w> their objection to the sale of medicines by doctors cannot be taken seriously.", "isC": true}], "feedback": "Correct. Rebuttal introduced by \'so\' at the very end."}, {"id": 17, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "Our radio station has a responsibility to serve the public interest. ", "isS": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "<i-w t=\\"Conclusion Indicator\\">Hence</i-w>, when our critics contend that our recent exposé of events in the private lives of local celebrities was excessively intrusive, ", "isO": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "we can only reply that the overwhelming public interest in these matters makes it our responsibility to publicize them.", "isC": true}], "feedback": "Correct. \'we can only reply that...\' is the rebuttal conclusion."}, {"id": 18, "type": "rebuttal", "parts": [{"id": "p1", "text": "The fire that destroyed the Municipal Building started before dawn this morning, and the last fire fighters did not leave until late this afternoon. ", "isS": true}, {"id": "p2", "text": "Thomas <i-w t=\\"Opinion Indicator\\">must</i-w> have seen it, ", "isC": true}, {"id": "p3", "text": "whatever he now says to the contrary. ## assistant

I rechecked both links live on 2026-09-14. Correction to my earlier count: that snapshot is stale. For the next six weekdays, **Tuesday, September 15 through Tuesday, September 22**, the current availability is:

| Date | Cal.com | LSAT Prep |
|---|---:|---:|
| Tue, Sep 15 | 3 slots | 3 |
| Wed, Sep 16 | 3 | 3 |
| Thu, Sep 17 | 3 | 2 |
| Fri, Sep 18 | 3 | 3 |
| Mon, Sep 21 | 3 | 3 |
| Tue, Sep 22 | 4 | 3 |
| **Six-weekday total** | **19** | **17** |

They’re similar in pattern, but not identical in times or count. Including the weekend, each link currently has **one Saturday slot at 8:00 p.m. and no Sunday slots**. That makes 20 slots on Cal.com and 18 on LSAT Prep across the full date range.

The booking questions are essentially the same: both collect name and email, with optional notes and guests. LSAT Prep also shows a timezone selector. I found no extra LSAT-specific screening question on either form. Both are listed as 70 minutes at $45. Cal’s configured fields are available through its [event-type API](https://cal.com/docs/api-reference/v2/event-types/get-an-event-type).

[LSAT Prep booking link](https://lsatprep.study/book/session) · [Cal.com booking link](https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/session-link)

I checked the available slots and form setup; I did not submit a booking.

Trigger on "assign <student> …", "post … to <student>", "message <student>", "send <student> homework", "give <student> an activity", or any request to make something happen on the platform from here.\n---\n\n# LSAT U Platform Actions\n\nJoshua\'s workplace is this brain workspace, not the app\'s admin UI. This skill makes\nClaude the operator: he says what should happen to a student on LSAT U, Claude drives\nthe deployed platform to make it happen, verifies it landed, and records it.\n\n## Mechanism\n\nUse the **claude-in-chrome** browser tools against `https://lsatprep.study` with\nJoshua\'s logged-in Chrome admin session. There is no agent path to the prod Supabase\nbackend (Lovable-managed, not in MCP scope) — the browser + admin JWT IS the API.\nDo not insert rows backend-style; act through the same UI flows Joshua would use,\nunless he explicitly asks for a backend repair.\n\n**React controlled-input rule (hard-won 2026-07-16):** `form_input` sets DOM values\nbut often does NOT update React state, leaving submit buttons silently disabled and\nproducing zero network traffic. For every form:\n\n1. Click the field and type with real keystrokes (`computer` type action); for\n   `<select>`s, click to focus then choose with keyboard (first-letter + Enter) or a\n   real option click.\n2. Before clicking submit, confirm the button is actually enabled (full-opacity, or\n   check `disabled` via read_page).\n3. After clicking, confirm via `read_network_requests` that the mutation actually\n   fired (POST to `…supabase.co/functions/v1/…` or `/rest/v1/…`) and returned 2xx.\n   A UI that merely looks done is not evidence.\n\n## Routing — where each action lives\n\nAll under `/teacher` (Teacher Desk). Workspaces via `?ws=`: `people` / `students`,\n`library`, `messages`/`inbox`, `admin`, `gmail`, `drive`.\n\n| Action | Surface |\n|---|---|\n| Assign a guided drill | `/teacher?ws=library&tab=guided-drills` → drill card → Assign. **Delegate to the `guided-drill-assignment` skill** for selection/QA/logging — it is the specialist; this skill is the router. |\n| Assign homework / quiz | Teacher Desk library or the student\'s detail page (`TeacherStudentDetail`) → assign flow → creates classroom item + notification |\n| Assign an activity (e.g. Negation Station) | Library activities surface → Assign; verify resume + notification (see `wiki/tasks.md` activity-centre notes) |\n| Post a classroom item / note | Student detail page → classroom/dossier tabs |\n| Message a student | `ws=messages` (admin side of the 1:1 inbox); supports PDF attachments |\n| Ingest a session transcript | `/teacher/session-ingest` (Phase 1A form: Doc URL + student + optional Gmail message id) or the scan/resolve queue on the same page |\n| Review/publish a session bundle | Student detail → dossier/review tabs (`SessionBundleReview`); publish is approval-gated |\n\nIf a flow\'s exact clicks are unknown, discover them live with read_page/find on the\nreal surface — do not guess selectors from memory, and do not invent UI that isn\'t\nthere.\n\n## Order of operations (every action)\n\n1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one\n   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and\n   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.\n2. **Do the action** through the UI per the routing table, honoring the React rule.\n3. **Verify it landed**: the mutation request returned 2xx AND the UI reflects it\n   (item appears in the student\'s classroom list / message thread / queue). For\n   assignments, verify the notification surface where checkable.\n4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with\n   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to\n   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see\n  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua\'s voice —\n  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\n- If Joshua\'s request fully specifies the action ("assign Lydia the conditional\n  drill"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he\n  hasn\'t dictated), draft it and show him before sending.\n- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn\'t\n  confirmed.
