This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_list_workspaces(args: {\\n  // Pagination cursor from a previous response.\\n  cursor?: string;\\n  // Max results (default 50, max 100)\\n  limit?: number;\\n  // Pagination offset (default 0)\\n  offset?: number;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ has_more?: boolean; pagination?: { has_more: boolean; next_cursor: string | null; [key: string]: unknown; }; workspaces: Array<{ id?: string; membership?: { role?: string; [key: string]: unknown; }; name?: string; num_projects?: number; plan?: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_move_projects_to_folder","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nMove one or more projects into a workspace folder. Existing folder memberships are removed first. The folder may update project visibility to match folder visibility; the response lists added, skipped, and visibility-updated projects. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_move_projects_to_folder(args: {\\n  // Target folder ID\\n  folder_id: string;\\n  // Project IDs to move into the target folder\\n  project_ids: Array<string>;\\n  // Workspace ID\\n  workspace_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ added_project_ids: Array<string> | null; skipped_projects: Array<{ project_id: string; reason: string; [key: string]: unknown; }> | null; total_added: number; total_requested: number; visibility_updated: Array<{ new_visibility: \\"public\\" | \\"private\\" | \\"draft\\" | \\"workspace_view\\"; previous_visibility?: \\"public\\" | \\"private\\" | \\"draft\\" | \\"workspace_view\\"; project_id: string; [key: string]: unknown; }> | null; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_query_database","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nExecute a SQL query against a project\'s cloud PostgreSQL database. Returns rows as JSON objects. Supports SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, and DDL statements. The database must be enabled first (use get_database_status to check, then enable_database if needed). Caution: write operations (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, DROP) modify production data permanently — prefer SELECT for inspection unless a write is explicitly required. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_query_database(args: {\\n  // Project ID\\n  project_id: string;\\n  // SQL query to execute\\n  sql: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ rows: Array<{ [key: string]: unknown; }> | null; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_read_file","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nRead the contents of a single file in a Lovable project. Optionally pass latest_commit_sha from get_project, or commit_sha from send_message/list_edits. Returns the raw file content as text. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_read_file(args: {\\n  // File path within the repository (e.g. \'src/App.tsx\')\\n  path: string;\\n  // Project ID\\n  project_id: string;\\n  // Git ref — commit SHA, branch name, or tag\\n  ref?: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_remix_project","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nRemix (fork) an existing project into a workspace. Creates a copy of the source project. Waits for the remix to complete and returns the new project details. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_remix_project(args: {\\n  // Copy custom knowledge (instructions) to the new project\\n  include_custom_knowledge?: boolean;\\n  // Copy chat history to the new project\\n  include_history?: boolean;\\n  // Source project ID to remix\\n  project_id: string;\\n  // Custom name for the new project\\n  project_name?: string;\\n  // Max seconds to wait for remix completion (default: 600, max: 600).\\n  timeout_seconds?: number;\\n  // Target workspace ID for the new project\\n  workspace_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ created_at?: string; description?: string; display_name?: string; editor_url?: string; error?: string; folder_id?: string; generated_description?: string; id?: string; is_published?: boolean; job_id: string; last_edited_at?: string; latest_commit_sha?: string; latest_screenshot_url?: string; name?: string; og_image_url?: string; preview_url?: string; project_type?: string; publish_visibility?: string; remix_status?: string; status?: string; tech_stack?: string; updated_at?: string; url?: string; user_id?: string; visibility?: string; workspace_id?: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_render_project_widget","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nRender the Lovable project status widget for a project that already exists. Call only after create_project returns projectId. Returns widget bootstrap data and does not create or modify projects. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_render_project_widget(args: {\\n  // Project ID returned by create_project.\\n  project_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ projectId: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_send_message","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nSend a chat message to a project\'s AI agent and wait for the agent to finish. The agent can write and edit code, install npm packages, create pages and components, set up authentication, connect integrations (Stripe, Supabase, etc.), and fix bugs. Write messages in natural language — describe what you want, not how to implement it. Attach images (designs, screenshots) to guide visual changes. Returns the agent\'s response, activity log, and any edits made. The preview_url is rebuilt once the agent finishes. Set wait=false to return immediately without waiting. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_send_message(args: {\\n  // Uploaded file references\\n  files?: Array<{\\n  // Uploaded file ID\\n  file_id: string;\\n  // Original file name\\n  file_name?: string;\\n  // MIME type\\n  mime_type?: string;\\n  // File type\\n  type?: string;\\n}>;\\n  // Run this turn in Max mode: the strongest model at high effort, 2.5x credits. Build turns only; ignored when plan_mode is set. Requires the max-mode feature or a Lovable-on-Lovable project; the API rejects the request with 403 otherwise (default: false)\\n  max_mode?: boolean;\\n  // Message text for the AI agent\\n  message: string;\\n  // Enable plan mode: the agent discusses and plans without editing code. Use for complex features, architectural decisions, or when you want to align on approach before changes are made (default: false)\\n  plan_mode?: boolean;\\n  // Project ID\\n  project_id: string;\\n  // Max seconds to wait for agent completion (default: 600, max: 600). If it times out, returns status \'in_progress\' with the message_id; use get_message to continue polling.\\n  timeout_seconds?: number;\\n  // Wait for the agent to finish processing (default: true)\\n  wait?: boolean;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ agent_version?: string; commit_sha?: string; content?: string; cost_credits?: number; deduplicated?: boolean; edit_id?: string; error?: string; message_id: string; preview_url?: string; status: string; summary?: string; thread_id?: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_folder_visibility","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nSet a folder\'s visibility. \'personal\' restricts to the creator and collaborators. \'workspace\' makes it visible to all workspace members. Changing visibility propagates to all projects in the folder. Nested folders cannot change visibility (move to root first). Personal folders require a Business or Enterprise plan. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_folder_visibility(args: {\\n  // Folder ID\\n  folder_id: string;\\n  // Folder visibility level\\n  visibility: \\"personal\\" | \\"workspace\\";\\n  // Workspace ID\\n  workspace_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ visibility: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_project_knowledge","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nSet a project\'s knowledge content. Knowledge is custom instructions that guide the AI agent\'s behavior for this specific project. Max 10,000 characters. Caution: this replaces existing knowledge entirely — read with get_project_knowledge first if preserving existing content. Examples of useful knowledge: coding conventions, preferred libraries, brand voice guidelines, API endpoint patterns, or \'always use Supabase for auth\'. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_project_knowledge(args: {\\n  // Knowledge content (markdown). Pass empty string to clear.\\n  content: string;\\n  // Project ID\\n  project_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ content: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_project_visibility","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nSet a project\'s visibility. \'private\' makes it editable by workspace members. \'workspace_view\' makes it visible (read-only) to workspace members (requires Business plan or higher). \'public\' makes it visible to anyone (not available on enterprise plans). \'draft\' makes it visible only to the creator (requires Business plan or higher). This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_project_visibility(args: {\\n  // Project ID\\n  project_id: string;\\n  // Project visibility level\\n  visibility: \\"draft\\" | \\"private\\" | \\"public\\" | \\"workspace_view\\";\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ visibility: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_workspace_knowledge","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nSet the workspace\'s knowledge content. Knowledge is custom instructions that guide the AI agent\'s behavior across all projects in the workspace. Max 10,000 characters. Caution: this replaces existing knowledge entirely — read with get_workspace_knowledge first if preserving existing content. Examples of useful knowledge: coding conventions, preferred libraries, brand voice guidelines, API endpoint patterns, or \'always use Supabase for auth\'. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_set_workspace_knowledge(args: {\\n  // Knowledge content (markdown). Pass empty string to clear.\\n  content: string;\\n  // Workspace ID\\n  workspace_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ content: string; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__lovable_update_workspace_skill","description":"Build full-stack apps by chat\\n\\nUpdate a workspace skill by replacing skills/{skill_name}/SKILL.md in the workspace repo. Only workspace admins and owners can update skills. Read the existing skill first with get_workspace_skill if you need to preserve content. This tool is part of plugin `Lovable`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__lovable_update_workspace_skill(args: {\\n  // Full replacement SKILL.md contents, including frontmatter\\n  markdown: string;\\n  // Skill name; must match the frontmatter name\\n  skill_name: string;\\n  // Workspace ID\\n  workspace_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{ commit_sha: string; skill: { description: string; files: Array<{ binary: boolean; contents?: string; name: string; sizeBytes: number; sizeExceeded: boolean; [key: string]: unknown; }> | null; markdown?: string; metadata?: { [key: string]: unknown; }; name: string; [key: string]: unknown; }; [key: string]: unknown; }>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_management_get_app_permissions","description":"Manage plugins, settings, permissions, and connections. Prefer available built-in tools or connected plugins when they fit the task. Proactively search for plugins when an external app, account, or service would materially help, even if the user did not request a plugin. Search before claiming a service is unavailable or suggesting manual workarounds. Do not suggest plugins for native web search, image generation, memory, or sites unless a specific external provider or missing capability is needed.\\n\\nInspect one named ChatGPT plugin\'s global/default and plugin-specific permission settings. Use when the user asks what the plugin may read, write, or do, whether it must ask first, or whether it inherits the default. For a missing/broad target such as my plugins, all, or Google, make no call and ask which plugin. Never pass global. Do not use for OAuth/admin scopes, install/connect/undo requests, ordinary plugin use, or npm/Chrome/code plugins. This tool is part of plugin `Plugin Management`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_management_get_app_permissions(args: {\\n  // ChatGPT plugin reference to inspect. May be a plugin id, connector id, platform slug, or unambiguous user-facing plugin name. It must identify one plugin; never pass all, global, Google, or another broad/generic target.\\n  app_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{\\n  // The server\'s response to a tool call.\\n  result: { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; content: Array<{ _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; text: string; type: \\"text\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; data: string; mimeType: string; type: \\"image\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; data: string; mimeType: string; type: \\"audio\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; description?: string | null; icons?: Array<{ mimeType?: string | null; sizes?: Array<string> | null; src: string; }> | null; mimeType?: string | null; name: string; size?: number | null; title?: string | null; type: \\"resource_link\\"; uri: string; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; resource: { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; mimeType?: string | null; text: string; uri: string; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; blob: string; mimeType?: string | null; uri: string; }; type: \\"resource\\"; }>; isError?: boolean; structuredContent?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; };\\n}>>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_management_uninstall_app","description":"Manage plugins, settings, permissions, and connections. Prefer available built-in tools or connected plugins when they fit the task. Proactively search for plugins when an external app, account, or service would materially help, even if the user did not request a plugin. Search before claiming a service is unavailable or suggesting manual workarounds. Do not suggest plugins for native web search, image generation, memory, or sites unless a specific external provider or missing capability is needed.\\n\\nUninstall ChatGPT plugins only for explicit uninstall, remove, or disconnect intent. Pass every exact, user-approved target in one call. For a missing/broad target such as Google, all/risky plugins, or a choice left to you, make no call and ask. Disable is not uninstall. Never use this for install/connect/undo/how-to, sentiment, negation, ordinary plugin use, or npm/Chrome/code plugins. The result reports each outcome. This tool is part of plugin `Plugin Management`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_management_uninstall_app(args: {\\n  // Exact, user-approved ChatGPT plugin references to uninstall. Each item may be a plugin id, connector id, platform slug, or unambiguous user-facing name. Never pass Google or another broad provider, all/risky plugins, or a target chosen by the assistant.\\n  app_ids: Array<string>;\\n  // Optional user-visible reason for uninstalling the plugin.\\n  reason?: string | null;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult<{\\n  // The server\'s response to a tool call.\\n  result: { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; content: Array<{ _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; text: string; type: \\"text\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; data: string; mimeType: string; type: \\"image\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; data: string; mimeType: string; type: \\"audio\\"; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; description?: string | null; icons?: Array<{ mimeType?: string | null; sizes?: Array<string> | null; src: string; }> | null; mimeType?: string | null; name: string; size?: number | null; title?: string | null; type: \\"resource_link\\"; uri: string; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; annotations?: { audience?: Array<\\"user\\" | \\"assistant\\"> | null; priority?: number | null; } | null; resource: { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; mimeType?: string | null; text: string; uri: string; } | { _meta?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; blob: string; mimeType?: string | null; uri: string; }; type: \\"resource\\"; }>; isError?: boolean; structuredContent?: { [key: string]: unknown; } | null; };\\n}>>; };\\n```"}]'}]

Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation."\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs\r\n\r\nHelp turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.\r\n\r\nStart by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you\'re building, present the design and get user approval.\r\n\r\n<HARD-GATE>\r\nDo NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.\r\n</HARD-GATE>\r\n\r\n## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"\r\n\r\nEvery project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change — all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.\r\n\r\n## Checklist\r\n\r\nYou MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:\r\n\r\n1. **Explore project context** — check files, docs, recent commits\r\n2. **Offer the visual companion just-in-time** — NOT upfront. The first time a question would genuinely be clearer shown than described, offer it then (its own message); on approval its browser tab opens for you. If no visual question ever arises, never offer it. See the Visual Companion section below.\r\n3. **Ask clarifying questions** — one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria\r\n4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** — with trade-offs and your recommendation\r\n5. **Present design** — in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section\r\n6. **Write design doc** — save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit\r\n7. **Spec self-review** — quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)\r\n8. **User reviews written spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding\r\n9. **Transition to implementation** — invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan\r\n\r\n## Process Flow\r\n\r\n```dot\r\ndigraph brainstorming {\r\n    "Explore project context" [shape=box];\r\n    "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];\r\n    "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];\r\n    "Present design sections" [shape=box];\r\n    "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];\r\n    "Write design doc" [shape=box];\r\n    "Spec self-review\\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];\r\n    "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];\r\n    "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];\r\n\r\n    "Explore project context" -> "Ask clarifying questions";\r\n    "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";\r\n    "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";\r\n    "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";\r\n    "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];\r\n    "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];\r\n    "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\\n(fix inline)";\r\n    "Spec self-review\\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";\r\n    "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];\r\n    "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.\r\n\r\n## The Process\r\n\r\n**Understanding the idea:**\r\n\r\n- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)\r\n- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don\'t spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.\r\n- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec → plan → implementation cycle.\r\n- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea\r\n- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too\r\n- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions\r\n- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria\r\n\r\n**Exploring approaches:**\r\n\r\n- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs\r\n- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning\r\n- Lead with your recommended option and explain why\r\n\r\n**Presenting the design:**\r\n\r\n- Once you believe you understand what you\'re building, present the design\r\n- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced\r\n- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far\r\n- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing\r\n- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn\'t make sense\r\n\r\n**Design for isolation and clarity:**\r\n\r\n- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently\r\n- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?\r\n- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.\r\n- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that\'s often a signal that it\'s doing too much.\r\n\r\n**Working in existing codebases:**\r\n\r\n- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.\r\n- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that\'s grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they\'re working in.\r\n- Don\'t propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.\r\n\r\n## After the Design\r\n\r\n**Documentation:**\r\n\r\n- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`\r\n  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)\r\n- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available\r\n- Commit the design document to git\r\n\r\n**Spec Self-Review:**\r\nAfter writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:\r\n\r\n1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.\r\n2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?\r\n3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?\r\n4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.\r\n\r\nFix any issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on.\r\n\r\n**User Review Gate:**\r\nAfter the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:\r\n\r\n> "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."\r\n\r\nWait for the user\'s response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.\r\n\r\n**Implementation:**\r\n\r\n- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan\r\n- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.\r\n\r\n## Key Principles\r\n\r\n- **One question at a time** - Don\'t overwhelm with multiple questions\r\n- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible\r\n- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs\r\n- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling\r\n- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on\r\n- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn\'t make sense\r\n\r\n## Visual Companion\r\n\r\nA browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool — not a mode. Accepting the companion means it\'s available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.\r\n\r\n**Offering the companion (just-in-time):** Do NOT offer it upfront. Wait until a question would genuinely be clearer shown than told — a real mockup / layout / diagram question, not merely a UI *topic*. The first time that happens, offer it then, as its own message:\r\n> "This next part might be easier if I show you — I can put together mockups, diagrams, and comparisons in a browser tab as we go. It\'s still new and can be token-intensive. Want me to? I\'ll open it for you."\r\n\r\n**This offer MUST be its own message.** Only the offer — no clarifying question, summary, or other content. Wait for the user\'s response. If they accept, start the server with `--open` so their browser opens to the first screen automatically. If they decline, continue text-only and don\'t offer again unless they raise it.\r\n\r\n**Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**\r\n\r\n- **Use the browser** for content that IS visual — mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs\r\n- **Use the terminal** for content that is text — requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions\r\n\r\nA question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question — use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question — use the browser.\r\n\r\nIf they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:\r\n`skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`\r\n\r\n'}]

Lock the browser editor group: right-click the editor tab -> `Lock Group`.\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\agent\\browser-layout.md:39:- Use the browser extension path in `docs/agent/locatorjs.md` for click-to-source.\r\ndecisions/log.md:29:## 2026-07-20 — The booking pass is the only record of owed sessions\r\ndecisions/log.md:30:- **Decision:** A booking pass is the sole record that sessions are owed to a student. **No pass means nothing is owed, by definition.** There is no reconciliation between Stripe package purchases and passes granted, and none will be built for now. A student with no pass is the normal case, not a gap: they schedule individual sessions through their Stripe-linked Cal.com link. Only a student owed sessions holds a pass. Revoked passes are dead links and are excluded everywhere; exhausted passes are live but owe nothing.\r\ndecisions/log.md:32:- **Context:** Grilled 2026-07-20. Corrects a modeling error shipped hours earlier, where the roster listed students without passes under "No booking pass — grant booking access before drafting anything," framing the normal case as a defect. Companion decisions the same day: contact truth is Gmail behaviour, never a pass\'s stored email (Ethan\'s pass carried an address with one message against it while he corresponded from another with 201); status stays authored but contradictions are surfaced (Yahunatan sat as `prospect` holding 9 owed sessions); one lifecycle path — prospect, active, lapsed — with no "not a student" category, since Yahunatan was a consultation on 07-07 and a paying student by 07-15; a taught session is what earns a `students/` folder; and arrangement is authored while the remaining count is regenerated from the backend, never typed by hand (the hand-written mirrors had drifted within a day). Vocabulary in `CONTEXT.md`.\r\ndecisions/log.md:60:- **Decision:** One `.env` at the root is the single store for API keys, passwords, and bank/account info. `.gitignore` keeps it out of Git. No `private/` tier, no macOS Keychain, no encrypted vault (the `records.sparsebundle` design is dead, not deferred). Government IDs stay outside the OS entirely. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` in the editor.\r\ndecisions/log.md:74:## 2026-07-20 | Consultation follow-ups use scheduled send with an intervention window\r\ndecisions/log.md:75:- **Decision:** For a prospect who completed a consultation and has not paid, schedule the follow-up for 3:00 PM Toronto time on the next calendar day, including Saturdays, Sundays, and statutory holidays. It sends automatically unless Joshua stops or changes it before the scheduled time. This is a bounded exception to the general rule that AI-generated outbound messages remain drafts.\r\ndecisions/log.md:76:- **Reasoning:** Joshua wants the follow-up to happen reliably without requiring an approval click, while retaining time to review, edit, or cancel it.\r\ndecisions/log.md:92:- **Reasoning:** Stripe and Stripe-linked Cal.com are the operational authorities for standard payments, so asking the prospect to report a payment the system can observe adds friction and creates duplicate state.\r\ndecisions/log.md:116:- **Decision:** Present the standard offers as $45/hour for a single session, $40/hour for the five-hour package, and $35/hour for the ten-hour package. Do not show package totals in the email.\r\ndecisions/log.md:117:- **Reasoning:** Joshua wants the prospect-facing comparison centered on the hourly rate. The automation must still verify the total charged by each link before it schedules or sends the message.\r\ndecisions/log.md:121:- **Decision:** Label the single-session action `Schedule here`. Label the five-hour and ten-hour package actions `Purchase here`.\r\ndecisions/log.md:122:- **Reasoning:** Scheduling and purchasing are different actions. The labels should tell the prospect what each link does before they click it.\r\ndecisions/log.md:127:- **D3 — Consultation email sends:** fixed pre-approved templates may auto-send via deterministic scheduler; anything AI-generated stays a Gmail draft for approval.\r\ndecisions/log.md:128:- **D4 — Operational state:** live systems are authority, the brain mirrors. Cal.com + Google Calendar = sessions; LSAT U Supabase = homework/dossier/publish; Stripe = payments; Gmail = correspondence. Backend wins on disagreement. Not redundant — by design.*\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\audits\\pf-day-redundancy.md:146:- **Open:** `Schedule`\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\audits\\pf-day-redundancy.md:158:- **Open:** `flaw-packet`, `Schedule`\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\audits\\pf-day-redundancy.md:170:- **Open:** `Schedule`\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0003-teacher-desk-embeds-ai-assistant.md:24:The Teacher Desk (Today) embeds the AI Teaching Assistant as a permanent center panel, reversing ADR 0001 / the teacher CONTEXT.md rule that the Desk "is not the assistant page" and that Today must avoid "oversized assistant panels." The desk becomes three zones: left = the Reply/Review/Assign task lists, center = the assistant (priority chips → chat → approval-gated action cards), right = a thin per-task context strip. Work happens in the chat: selecting a task or clicking a priority chip seeds the assistant, which drafts a reply/recap/assignment that Joshua approves or rewrites on the card. The per-mode manual editors (reply textarea, draft editor, assign drill-picker) are removed from the desk; their function moves into the cards. There is one assistant with one shared conversation history (`operator_messages`), powered by the existing `teacher-operator-chat` engine, openable from the desk and (optionally) its dedicated home.\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:1:# 0005 — Single Session goes native, slot-first with a payment Hold\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:8:The booking-unification plan (2026-08-13) locked "single session stays on Cal.com; no Stripe single-session product." Since then: (a) the native booking core (availability, slot engine, Google Calendar + Meet creation, confirmation email) has proven itself for Packages; (b) the Cal.com account has zero webhooks configured, so Cal bookings never reach the app — they surface as "Nothing booked" on every student page; (c) Joshua wants one machine, not a mind split across two systems.\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:10:Two payment orderings were considered for a native Single Session:\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:12:1. **Pay-first (1-credit pass):** buy via Payment Link, webhook grants a pass, booker then books on their pass link. Least new code, but a Stripe Payment Link\'s success redirect is static, so the buyer must leave the flow and find an email to finish — worse than the Cal.com experience being replaced.\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:13:2. **Slot-first with a Hold:** pick a slot → enter details → a Hold blocks the slot → pay on Stripe\'s hosted page → payment confirmation finalizes the booking through the existing booking core.\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:17:Option 2. The public Single Session page reproduces Cal.com\'s order of operations exactly: slot → details → pay → confirmed. New machinery is the Hold (creation, blocking, expiry) and the payment-confirmation path; downstream side effects (calendar event with Meet link, schedule mirror, transactional emails) reuse the package-booking core\'s helpers. The Single Session is recorded as its own first-class booking record — per the domain glossary it needs no Booking Pass, and the pass invariants (one resumable pass per student, credit-restoring cancellation) must not apply to it. _(Amended 2026-08-16 after cross-model review: an earlier draft modeled it as a synthetic 1-credit pass; that violated both glossary and schema and was dropped.)_\r\nprojects/lsatu/docs\\adr\\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md:19:This explicitly supersedes decision #2 of `plans/booking-unification.md`. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: booking-link\ndescription: Use whenever Joshua wants a one-off booking link for LSAT U — "make me a link for Dakota at 7:15 tomorrow", "send her a link to book tonight", "one-off link, $60", "short notice link", "I need a link she can schedule herself with". Mints a single-use URL that sells exactly one session at a price you set, optionally pinned to one exact time, and closes after she books. Also covers listing and revoking existing links.\n---\n\n# One-off booking links\n\nA URL Joshua sends to one person. She opens it, books one session, pays, and\ngets the Google Meet invite from the ordinary booking flow. Then the link\ncloses.\n\n## Authorization\n\nMinting a link is pre-authorized. Joshua asking for a link means make it now —\ndo not ask permission, ask only for the facts you actually need.\n\n**Never send the link anywhere.** Print it and hand it to Joshua. He decides who\ngets it. A link is a Bearer [REDACTED]: whoever holds it can book that slot.\n\n## What you need before running\n\nOnly two things are required, and one has a default:\n\n- **Price** in dollars. Required — links always charge, and the standard rate is\n  $45 but a given person may pay something else. Ask if it was not said.\n- **Who it is for.** Used only for the admin-side note, never in the URL. If\n  Joshua did not say, use a short description.\n\nEverything else is optional. Ask for nothing you were not given.\n\n## Running it\n\nFrom `~/ai-os/projects/lsatu`:\n\n```bash\nnpm run booking:link -- --for "Dakota" --price 45 --at "2026-09-10 19:15"\n```\n\nTimes in `--at` are wall-clock in Joshua\'s booking timezone (`America/Toronto`),\nformat `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm`, 24-hour. Convert "7:15pm tomorrow" yourself — resolve\nrelative dates against today before running, and never pass a relative word\nthrough.\n\nIt prints the URL, the price, the time, and how many times the link can sell.\nGive Joshua the URL exactly as printed.\n\n### The flags\n\n| Flag | Default | What it does |\n|---|---|---|\n| `--for` | — | Who the link is for. Note only, never in the URL. |\n| `--price` | — | Dollars. Required. |\n| `--at` | none | Pin one exact time. See below. |\n| `--minutes` | 70 | Session length. |\n| `--notice` | 1440, or 0 with `--at` | Minimum notice in minutes. `0` means "bookable right now". |\n| `--window` | 6 | How many business days ahead the picker reaches. |\n| `--label` | "LSAT Session" | The title she sees on the page. |\n| `--note` | auto | Admin reminder of who and why. |\n\n### Pinned versus picker\n\n**`--at` pins one time.** She sees only that time and confirms it. The minimum\nnotice, the rolling window and the weekly hours are all skipped, because Joshua\nchoosing the time *is* that decision. This is the right shape for "I have a gap\nat 7:15 tonight, let her take it."\n\n**Without `--at` she gets a calendar** of open times, still inside the weekly\nhours. Use `--notice 0` for a short-notice link she can book today, and\n`--window` to control how far ahead it reaches.\n\nDouble-booking is never skipped either way. A pinned time that clashes with\nsomething already on the calendar is refused at mint time — the script deletes\nthe link and tells you what it hit — and refused again inside the booking\ntransaction.\n\n## Listing and revoking\n\n```bash\nnpm run booking:link -- --list\nnpm run booking:link -- --revoke <slug>\n```\n\n`--list` shows every one-off link with its state: `open`, `used`, or `revoked`.\nRevoking is immediate and the URL stops resolving.\n\n## Reading the outcome honestly\n\n- **"clashes with something already on your calendar"** — the time is taken. No\n  link was created. Tell Joshua what he asked for is not free and offer to pick\n  another time; do not silently mint a different one.\n- **"would have no open times at all"** — a picker link whose window and weekly\n  hours produce nothing. Usually `--window` is too small or the hours are empty.\n- **A time in the past** is refused. If Joshua asks for a time that has already\n  gone by today, say so rather than rolling it to tomorrow on your own.\n\n## How it works, so you can debug it\n\nA one-off link is a row in `booking_event_types` with a random 10-character slug\nand `single_use` set. That is the same table defining the ordinary session and\nthe consultation, and every layer beneath — the page, the `single-session` edge\nfunction, the SQL slot policy, the claim transaction — is already keyed by event\nslug. So the link books through exactly the code a normal session books through.\nThere is no second booking path to keep in sync.\n\nSingle use is enforced by the `enforce_booking_event_shape` trigger under a\nper-slug advisory lock, so two people opening the same link at the same moment\ncannot both get through. A hold holds the link; letting the checkout expire\nreleases it; a cancelled or refunded booking stays spent, and a replacement\nneeds a fresh link.\n\nRelevant files:\n\n- `scripts/booking-link.ts` — the CLI\n- `src/pages/OneOffBooking.tsx` and the `/book/x/:slug` route in `src/App.tsx`\n- `supabase/migrations/20260908120000_one_off_booking_links.sql`\n- `supabase/functions/single-session/index.ts`\n\n## Credentials\n\n`LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is\ngit-ignored. This repo\'s `.env` is tracked in git — never put a credential\nthere.\n\r\n---\nname: booking-link\ndescription: Use whenever Joshua wants a one-off booking link for LSAT U — "make me a link for Dakota at 7:15 tomorrow", "send her a link to book tonight", "one-off link, $60", "short notice link", "I need a link she can schedule herself with". This repo\'s `.env` is tracked in git — never put a credential\nthere.\n\r\n# 0005 — Single Session goes native, slot-first with a payment Hold\r\n\r\nDate: 2026-08-16\r\nStatus: accepted\r\n\r\n## Context\r\n\r\nThe booking-unification plan (2026-08-13) locked "single session stays on Cal.com; no Stripe single-session product." Since then: (a) the native booking core (availability, slot engine, Google Calendar + Meet creation, confirmation email) has proven itself for Packages; (b) the Cal.com account has zero webhooks configured, so Cal bookings never reach the app — they surface as "Nothing booked" on every student page; (c) Joshua wants one machine, not a mind split across two systems.\r\n\r\nTwo payment orderings were considered for a native Single Session:\r\n\r\n1. **Pay-first (1-credit pass):** buy via Payment Link, webhook grants a pass, booker then books on their pass link. Least new code, but a Stripe Payment Link\'s success redirect is static, so the buyer must leave the flow and find an email to finish — worse than the Cal.com experience being replaced.\r\n2. **Slot-first with a Hold:** pick a slot → enter details → a Hold blocks the slot → pay on Stripe\'s hosted page → payment confirmation finalizes the booking through the existing booking core.\r\n\r\n## Decision\r\n\r\nOption 2. The public Single Session page reproduces Cal.com\'s order of operations exactly: slot → details → pay → confirmed. New machinery is the Hold (creation, blocking, expiry) and the payment-confirmation path; downstream side effects (calendar event with Meet link, schedule mirror, transactional emails) reuse the package-booking core\'s helpers. Cal.com\'s `session-link` stays live in parallel until Joshua retires it manually.\r\n\r\n## Consequences\r\n\r\n- A $45 booker never leaves the flow to check email; parity with Cal.com is preserved.\r\n- Holds introduce state that can expire mid-payment; the edge cases (payment lands after expiry, slot re-taken) must be specified and tested.\r\n- The known "Cal booking invisible in app" defect disappears for single sessions, because the booking is native from birth.\r\n- The Cal.com webhook receiver branch in `booking-commerce-webhook` remains dead code until Cal is retired or a webhook is finally registered.\r\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{\n  "scripts": {\n    "booking:link": "vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts --",\n    "test:booking:edge": "deno test supabase/functions/_shared/bookingCore.test.ts",\n    "test:booking:db": "scripts/test-booking-db.sh",\n    "test:booking": "npm run test:booking:edge && npm run test:booking:db && vitest run src/lib/bookingSlots.test.ts src/lib/bookingExpiry.test.ts src/lib/bookingPassLinks.test.ts src/lib/bookingPhase1Contract.test.ts src/lib/bookingRemediationContract.test.ts",\n    "verify:no-canonical": "node scripts/knowledge/verify-no-canonical-in-build.mjs"\n  },\n  "dependencies": {\n    "@supabase/supabase-js": "^2.101.1"\n  },\n  "devDependencies": {}\n}\n/**\r\n * One-off booking links: mint a URL that sells exactly one session, at a price\r\n * you set, and then stops working.\r\n *\r\n * A one-off link is not a new booking system. It is a row in\r\n * `booking_event_types` -- the same table that defines the ordinary session and\r\n * the consultation -- with a random slug and `single_use` set. Every layer\r\n * beneath it (the page, the edge function, the slot policy, the claim\r\n * transaction) is already keyed by event slug, so the link books through\r\n * exactly the code a normal session books through.\r\n *\r\n * Two knobs make it useful for short notice:\r\n *\r\n *   --at      pins one exact time. The link offers only that time, and the\r\n *             notice, window and weekly-hours rules do not apply -- you already\r\n *             decided this hour is fine by choosing it. A genuine clash with\r\n *             your calendar still blocks it, both here and at booking time.\r\n *   --notice  how far ahead someone must book, in minutes. 0 means "any time\r\n *             from now". Only meaningful without --at.\r\n *\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --for "Dakota" --price 45 --at "2026-09-10 18:00"\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --for "Dakota" --price 60 --notice 0 --window 3\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --list\r\n *   npx vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --revoke session-x7k2m9qp4rtv\r\n *\r\n * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo\'s\r\n * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/assign.ts.\r\n */\r\nimport fs from "node:fs";\r\nimport path from "node:path";\r\nimport { randomInt } from "node:crypto";\r\nimport { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";\r\nimport { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";\r\n\r\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");\r\nconst APP_ORIGIN = "https://lsatprep.study";\r\n\r\nfunction readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {\r\n  const out: Record<string, string> = {};\r\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\r\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\r\n    const t = line.trim();\r\n    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;\r\n    const i = t.indexOf("=");\r\n    if (i < 0) continue;\r\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["\']|["\']$/g, "");\r\n  }\r\n  return out;\r\n}\r\n\r\nconst env = {\r\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),\r\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),\r\n};\r\n\r\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments\r\n\r\nfunction arg(name: string): string | undefined {\r\n  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);\r\n  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;\r\n}\r\nconst has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);\r\n\r\nconst USAGE =\r\n  "usage: vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- \\\\\\n" +\r\n  "         --for <name> --price <dollars> [--at \\"YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm\\"] [--minutes 70]\\\\\\n" +\r\n  "         [--notice <minutes>] [--window <business days>] [--label <text>] [--note <text>]\\\\\\n" +\r\n  "       vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --list\\\\\\n" +\r\n  "       vite-node scripts/booking-link.ts -- --revoke <slug>";\r\n\r\n// ------------------------------------------------------------------- timezone\r\n\r\n/** How far `tz` is from UTC at that instant, in milliseconds. */\r\nfunction tzOffsetMs(tz: string, date: Date): number {\r\n  const parts = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", {\r\n    timeZone: tz, hour12: false,\r\n    year: "numeric", month: "2-digit", day: "2-digit",\r\n    hour: "2-digit", minute: "2-digit", second: "2-digit",\r\n  }).formatToParts(date);\r\n  const at = Object.fromEntries(parts.map((part) => [part.type, part.value])) as Record<string, string>;\r\n  const asUtc = Date.UTC(+at.year, +at.month - 1, +at.day, +at.hour % 24, +at.minute, +at.second);\r\n  return asUtc - date.getTime();\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * A wall-clock time in Joshua\'s timezone, as a real instant. Done in two passes\r\n * because the offset itself depends on the instant: a naive single pass lands an\r\n * hour out on the two days a year the clocks move.\r\n */\r\nfunction hostWallClockToUtc(tz: string, text: string): Date | null {\r\n  const match = /^(\\d{4})-(\\d{2})-(\\d{2})[T ](\\d{1,2}):(\\d{2})$/.exec(text.trim());\r\n  if (!match) {\r\n    const iso = new Date(text);\r\n    return Number.isFinite(iso.getTime()) ? iso : null;\r\n  }\r\n  const [, y, mo, d, h, mi] = match;\r\n  const naive = Date.UTC(+y, +mo - 1, +d, +h, +mi);\r\n  const first = naive - tzOffsetMs(tz, new Date(naive));\r\n  const second = naive - tzOffsetMs(tz, new Date(first));\r\n  return new Date(second);\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction inHostTz(tz: string, iso: string): string {\r\n  return new Date(iso).toLocaleString("en-US", {\r\n    timeZone: tz, weekday: "short", month: "short", day: "numeric",\r\n    hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit", timeZoneName: "short",\r\n  });\r\n}\r\n\r\n// ----------------------------------------------------------------- the slug\r\n\r\n// No 0/O/1/l/i: these get read aloud and typed by hand.\r\nconst ALPHABET = "abcdefghjkmnpqrstuvwxyz23456789";\r\n\r\nfunction randomTail(length = 12): string {\r\n  let out = "";\r\n  for (let i = 0; i < length; i++) out += ALPHABET[randomInt(ALPHABET.length)];\r\n  return out;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Short enough to send in a text, and nothing but the secret.\r\n *\r\n * The name is deliberately not in the slug. It would lengthen a URL that gets\r\n * read aloud and retyped, and it would put a student\'s name in anything that\r\n * logs the path. Who a link is for is kept in `note`, admin-side.\r\n */\r\nfunction slugFor(): string {\r\n  return randomTail(10);\r\n}\r\n\r\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------- signin\r\n\r\nasync function signIn(): Promise<void> {\r\n  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;\r\n  const password = [REDACTED];\r\n  if (!email || !password) {\r\n    console.error(\r\n      "booking-link: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set.\\n" +\r\n        "  Add them to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). This repo\'s .env is TRACKED in git,\\n" +\r\n        "  so a credential placed there would be committed.",\r\n    );\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n  const { error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });\r\n  if (error) {\r\n    console.error(`booking-link: sign-in failed: ${error.message}`);\r\n    process.exit(1);\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\nasync function hostTimezone(): Promise<string> {\r\n  const { data } = await supabase.from("booking_availability_config").select("timezone").eq("id", 1).maybeSingle();\r\n  return data?.timezone || "America/Toronto";\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * The times this link can actually sell, asked of the booking engine itself.\r\n *\r\n * This is deliberately not a reimplementation of the availability rules. The\r\n * `slots` action unions the mirrored calendar, live Google free/busy and every\r\n * held session -- none of which is reachable from a script -- so asking it is\r\n * the only way to know a pinned time is genuinely free. An empty answer for a\r\n * pinned link means exactly one thing: that hour is already taken.\r\n */\r\nasync function slotsFor(slug: string): Promise<{ start_at: string }[]> {\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("single-session", {\r\n    body: { action: "slots", event: slug },\r\n  });\r\n  if (error) throw new Error(error.message);\r\n  if ((data as { error?: string })?.error) throw new Error((data as { error: string }).error);\r\n  return ((data as { slots?: { start_at: string }[] }).slots) ?? [];\r\n}\r\n\r\n// --------------------------------------------------------------------- modes\r\n\r\nasync function list(tz: string): Promise<void> {\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n    .from("booking_event_types")\r\n    .select("slug,label,length_minutes,price_cents,active,pinned_start_at,note,created_at")\r\n    .eq("single_use", true)\r\n    .order("created_at", { ascending: false });\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n  if (!data?.length) {\r\n    console.log("No one-off links yet.");\r\n    return;\r\n  }\r\n  for (const row of data) {\r\n    const spent = await supabase.rpc("booking_event_is_spent", { _event_slug: row.slug });\r\n    const state = !row.active ? "revoked" : spent.data === true ? "used" : "open";\r\n    console.log(\r\n      `${state.padEnd(8)} ${`$${(row.price_cents / 100).toFixed(2)}`.padEnd(9)} ${String(row.length_minutes).padStart(3)}m  ` +\r\n        `${row.pinned_start_at ? inHostTz(tz, row.pinned_start_at) : "any open time"}\\n` +\r\n        `         ${APP_ORIGIN}/book/x/${row.slug}${row.note ? `\\n         ${row.note}` : ""}\\n`,\r\n    );\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\nasync function revoke(slug: string): Promise<void> {\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n    .from("booking_event_types")\r\n    .update({ active: false, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() })\r\n    .eq("slug", slug)\r\n    .eq("single_use", true)\r\n    .select("slug")\r\n    .maybeSingle();\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n  if (!data) {\r\n    console.error(`booking-link: no one-off link with slug "${slug}".`);\r\n    process.exit(1);\r\n  }\r\n  console.log(`Revoked. ${APP_ORIGIN}/book/x/${slug} no longer resolves.`);\r\n}\r\n\r\nasync function create(tz: string): Promise<void> {\r\n  const forName = arg("for");\r\n  const priceText = arg("price");\r\n  if (!forName || !priceText) {\r\n    console.error(USAGE);\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const price = Number(priceText);\r\n  if (!Number.isFinite(price) || price <= 0) {\r\n    console.error("--price must be a positive number of dollars, e.g. --price 45");\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n  const priceCents = Math.round(price * 100);\r\n\r\n  const minutes = Number(arg("minutes") ?? 70);\r\n  if (!Number.isInteger(minutes) || minutes <= 0) {\r\n    console.error("--minutes must be a whole number of minutes");\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const atText = arg("at");\r\n  let pinnedStartAt: string | null = null;\r\n  if (atText) {\r\n    const pinned = hostWallClockToUtc(tz, atText);\r\n    if (!pinned) {\r\n      console.error(`--at must be "YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm" (in ${tz}) or a full ISO timestamp, got "${atText}"`);\r\n      process.exit(2);\r\n    }\r\n    if (pinned.getTime() <= Date.now()) {\r\n      console.error(`--at is in the past: ${inHostTz(tz, pinned.toISOString())}`);\r\n      process.exit(2);\r\n    }\r\n    pinnedStartAt = pinned.toISOString();\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  // A pinned link has already answered the notice question, so the column only\r\n  // matters for a picker link. Without --at, the default stays the ordinary 24\r\n  // hours rather than silently loosening every link that omits the flag.\r\n  const notice = Number(arg("notice") ?? (pinnedStartAt ? 0 : 1440));\r\n  const window = Number(arg("window") ?? 6);\r\n  if (!Number.isInteger(notice) || notice < 0 || !Number.isInteger(window) || window <= 0) {\r\n    console.error("--notice must be >= 0 minutes and --window a positive number of business days");\r\n    process.exit(2);\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const slug = slugFor();\r\n  const row = {\r\n    slug,\r\n    label: arg("label") ?? "LSAT Session",\r\n    length_minutes: minutes,\r\n    slot_interval_minutes: null,\r\n    minimum_notice_minutes: notice,\r\n    buffer_before_minutes: 0,\r\n    buffer_after_minutes: 0,\r\n    window_business_days: window,\r\n    price_cents: priceCents,\r\n    currency: "usd",\r\n    requires_payment: true,\r\n    active: true,\r\n    single_use: true,\r\n    pinned_start_at: pinnedStartAt,\r\n    note: arg("note") ?? `One-off link for ${forName}.`,\r\n  };\r\n\r\n  const { error } = await supabase.from("booking_event_types").insert(row);\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n\r\n  // Ask the booking engine what this link can sell. A pinned link that comes\r\n  // back empty clashes with something already on the calendar, and a link that\r\n  // would fail the moment it was opened is worse than no link -- so it is\r\n  // deleted rather than handed over. Nothing has seen the URL yet.\r\n  let slots: { start_at: string }[];\r\n  try {\r\n    slots = await slotsFor(slug);\r\n  } catch (problem) {\r\n    await supabase.from("booking_event_types").delete().eq("slug", slug);\r\n    throw problem;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  if (!slots.length) {\r\n    await supabase.from("booking_event_types").delete().eq("slug", slug);\r\n    console.error(\r\n      pinnedStartAt\r\n        ? `booking-link: ${inHostTz(tz, pinnedStartAt)} clashes with something already on your calendar. No link was created.`\r\n        : "booking-link: this link would have no open times at all (check your weekly hours and window). No link was created.",\r\n    );\r\n    process.exit(1);\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const url = `${APP_ORIGIN}/book/x/${slug}`;\r\n  console.log(`\\n  ${url}\\n`);\r\n  console.log(`  ${forName} · $${(priceCents / 100).toFixed(2)} · ${minutes} minutes`);\r\n  console.log(\r\n    pinnedStartAt\r\n      ? `  Books ${inHostTz(tz, pinnedStartAt)} and only that time.`\r\n      : `  ${slots.length} open time${slots.length === 1 ? "" : "s"}, first ${inHostTz(tz, slots[0].start_at)}` +\r\n          `${notice === 0 ? " (no minimum notice)" : ` (${notice} minutes\' notice)`}.`,\r\n  );\r\n  console.log("  Sells one session, then closes.\\n");\r\n}\r\n\r\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------- main\r\n\r\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\r\n  await signIn();\r\n  const tz = await hostTimezone();\r\n  const revokeSlug = arg("revoke");\r\n  if (has("list")) return list(tz);\r\n  if (revokeSlug) return revoke(revokeSlug);\r\n  return create(tz);\r\n}\r\n\r\nmain()\r\n  .then(() => process.exit(0))\r\n  .catch((problem) => {\r\n    console.error(`booking-link: ${problem instanceof Error ? problem.message : String(problem)}`);\r\n    process.exit(1);\r\n  });\r\n\r\n M .env.example\n M .gitignore\n M [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Leaving Cal.com — spec and plan\n\n**Written 2026-09-09.** Goal: every booking Joshua takes happens on LSAT U, and the\nCal.com account is closed.\n\nEvery number in the "Cal.com today" tables was read live from the Cal.com API v2\non 2026-09-09, and every number in the "LSAT U today" tables was read from the\nproduction database on the same day. Nothing here is remembered or assumed. Where\nsomething is unverified it says so.\n\n---\n\n## The headline\n\nThis is a smaller job than it looks. Cal.com has twelve event types, and **ten of\nthem are the same session sold to one person at their own price and length.**\nLSAT U already models that as data rather than as twelve hand-made pages, so most\nof the migration is rows, not features.\n\nWhat is genuinely missing is five things, and none is large:\n\n1. Per-event schedules (the consultation runs on different hours).\n2. Date overrides (a one-off evening opened for one student).\n3. A booking window measured in calendar days, not business days.\n4. Emailing a link from the platform.\n5. Reminders, and student-initiated reschedule.\n\n---\n\n## Ground truth: Cal.com today\n\n### The twelve event types\n\nAll twelve place their meeting through the Google Meet integration. None uses\nseats, recurrence, or requires-confirmation, so none of that needs building.\n\n| Slug | Title | Min | Price | Buffers | Notice | Public |\n|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|\n| `consultation` | Consultation | 30 | free | 30 / 30 | 24 h | **yes** |\n| `session-link` | LSAT Session | 70 | $45.00 | 0 / 0 | 24 h | no |\n| `sabrina` | Sabrina | 90 | $60.00 | 15 / 15 | 24 h | no |\n| `rr` | Reduced link | 75 | $56.00 | 15 / 15 | 24 h | no |\n| `booking-reduced-rate` | Booking (Reduced Rate) | 90 | $52.50 | 15 / 15 | 10 h | no |\n| `session` | Session | 60 | $40.00 | 0 / 0 | 6 h | no |\n| `reduced-rate` | Reduced rate | 70 | $40.00 | 0 / 0 | 48 h | no |\n| `tutoring` | Dakota | 60 | $55.00 | 10 / 10 | 24 h | no |\n| `c-reduced-rate` | Reduced Rate | 70 | $35.00 | 10 / 10 | 48 h | no |\n| `canadian-scheduling` | Canadian Scheduling 🇨🇦 | 70 | $61.00 | 10 / 10 | 48 h | no |\n| `complimentary-session` | Complimentary session | 45 | free | 0 / 0 | 2 h | no |\n| `lsat` | LSAT | 65 | $60.00 | 0 / 0 | 2 h | no |\n\nSix distinct lengths are in use: 30, 45, 60, 65, 70, 75, 90.\n\n### The two schedules\n\n**"Working Hours"** (id 681247, default, America/Toronto) — Mon 14:00–20:00,\nTue 13:00–19:30, Wed 13:00–19:00, Thu 13:00–19:30, Fri 13:00–19:30,\nSat 14:00–17:00. Sunday closed. Carries date overrides.\n\n**"Consultation"** (id 696906, America/Toronto) — **all seven days, 13:30–20:00.**\n\nThis second schedule is the thing most likely to be missed. The consultation is\ndeliberately easier to book than a session: wider hours, and Sundays open.\n\n### Other live behaviour\n\n- Payment is taken **on booking**. The Stripe page holds the slot 30 minutes and\n  then dies unrecoverably, stranding the slot. See [[CALCOM-STUCK-BOOKINGS]].\n- Booking window was widened 2026-09-08 from 6 business days to **45 calendar\n  days**, because students could see barely a week and at most one Saturday.\n- At least one date override is live: 2026-09-19, 20:00–21:10 ET, for one student.\n\n---\n\n## Ground truth: LSAT U today\n\n### Weekly hours — already correct\n\n`booking_availability`, timezone `America/Toronto`, matches Cal.com\'s Working\nHours exactly, day for day:\n\n| Day | LSAT U | Cal.com Working Hours |\n|---|---|---|\n| Mon | 14:00–20:00 | 14:00–20:00 |\n| Tue | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Wed | 13:00–19:00 | 13:00–19:00 |\n| Thu | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Fri | 13:00–19:30 | 13:00–19:30 |\n| Sat | 14:00–17:00 | 14:00–17:00 |\n| Sun | closed | closed |\n\n### Event types\n\n| Slug | Min | Price | Buffers | Notice | Window |\n|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|\n| `single-session` | 70 | $45.00 | 0 / 0 | 24 h | 6 business days |\n| `consultation` | 30 | free | 30 / 30 | 24 h | 5 business days |\n\n### What already works, and should not be rebuilt\n\n- Slot generation, weekly hours, buffers, per-event notice and slot pitch.\n- Google Calendar: live free/busy, event creation, Meet link, mirrored events.\n- Stripe checkout, holds, expiry sweeper, refunds, cancellation with tokens.\n- Booking confirmation and cancellation/refund emails.\n- Per-person pricing by email (`booking_price_overrides`).\n- One-off single-use links, optionally pinned to an exact time (shipped\n  2026-09-08, `booking-link` skill).\n- A hold model that is **better than Cal.com\'s**: LSAT U holds the slot, expires\n  it, and sweeps it back. The stuck-booking failure that loses Joshua sessions\n  today is a Cal.com defect that does not exist here.\n\n---\n\n## The gaps\n\nSeven items. Each says what is missing and what it costs.\n\n### 1. Per-event schedules — blocks the consultation\n\nCal.com\'s consultation runs on its own hours (7 days, 13:30–20:00). LSAT U has\nexactly one weekly-hours table, so a consultation on LSAT U would inherit\nsession hours and lose Sundays and its earlier start.\n\nNeeds: a `booking_schedules` table, `booking_availability.schedule_id`, and a\n`booking_event_types.schedule_id`, defaulting to the current rows so nothing\nmoves. Two SQL functions read availability and must take the schedule.\n\n### 2. Date overrides — a live feature with a booking already on it\n\nCal.com has at least one override open right now. LSAT U has no concept of one,\nso migrating today would silently drop it.\n\nNeeds: a `booking_date_overrides` table (date, optional window, or closed), read\nby the slot policy and the generator. A closed override must also refuse a\nbooking that is already pinned to that day.\n\n### 3. Booking window unit mismatch\n\nLSAT U counts **6 business days**; Cal.com now uses **45 calendar days**. Ported\nas-is, students would again see about a week — the exact problem Joshua fixed on\nCal.com the day before.\n\nNeeds: `window_calendar_days` alongside, or replacing, `window_business_days`.\nRecommend replacing: business days were a workaround, and calendar days are what\nthe product actually wants.\n\n### 4. Emailing a link\n\nAsked for directly. Today `booking-link` prints a URL to the terminal.\n\nNeeds: an `--email` flag that renders a short template through the existing\n`send-transactional-email` function. Everything underneath exists; this is a\ntemplate plus a flag. **The email must be a draft-and-confirm step, not a silent\nsend** — house rule.\n\n### 5. Reminders — not built\n\nCal.com sends them. LSAT U sends nothing between confirmation and the session.\nVerified: no reminder code exists anywhere in the repo.\n\nNeeds: a scheduled sweep over confirmed bookings plus a reminder template.\nNon-trivial but well-bounded, and it reuses the outbox worker pattern the single\nsession already has.\n\n### 6. Student-initiated reschedule — not built for single sessions\n\n`student-booking` has reschedule for the pass flow; the single-session path has\ncancel only. Cal.com lets an attendee reschedule from the confirmation email.\n\nNeeds: a `reschedule` action re-running the slot claim under the existing cancel\ntoken, moving the Google event rather than making a second one.\n\n### 7. Ten personal event types to move\n\nEach is a person with their own length and price. LSAT U can express this today\nas a `booking_event_types` row per student with `single_use = false` — the same\nshape Cal.com uses, minus the hand-made page. `booking_price_overrides` covers\nprice but not length, so per-person **length** is what makes a row necessary.\n\nNeeds: no new schema. A `--personal` mode on the CLI, and ten rows.\n\n---\n\n## Decisions needed from Joshua\n\nNothing below is a technical unknown; each is a product call that changes what\ngets built.\n\n1. **Booking window:** replace business days with calendar days, and set it to 45\n   to match Cal.com? *(Recommend yes.)*\n2. **Personal link slugs:** random and unguessable, or readable like\n   `/book/x/dakota`? Readable is friendlier and is what Cal.com does; random\n   means the URL alone is the credential. *(Recommend readable for standing\n   student links, random for one-offs.)*\n3. **Reminders:** wanted at cutover, or after? If wanted, at what offsets — Cal.com\'s\n   defaults are 24 h and 1 h.\n4. **Reschedule:** may a student reschedule themselves, or does that stay with\n   Joshua? Today, on LSAT U, it is Joshua\'s.\n5. **Existing future Cal.com bookings:** migrate them into LSAT U, or let them\n   run out on Cal.com and stop taking new ones there?\n6. **The consultation is the public front door.** It is the only unhidden Cal.com\n   event. Moving it changes what a stranger from the website meets, so it should\n   move last and be watched.\n\n---\n\n## Plan\n\nEach phase ends in something verifiable. No phase depends on a later one.\n\n### Phase 0 — done (2026-09-08)\n\nEvent types as data; one-off single-use links; pinned times; the 70-minute and\n$45 CHECK constraints removed. Shipped in `7ef4861`, migration applied, function\ndeployed.\n\n**Note:** this also unblocked the consultation, which could not be booked at all —\nits 30-minute insert violated a 70-minute constraint. Unverified live.\n\n### Phase 1 — parity of the rules (no new surfaces)\n\n1. Calendar-day booking window, set to 45.\n2. `booking_schedules` + `schedule_id` on availability and event types; seed\n   "Working Hours" and "Consultation" from the values in this document.\n3. `booking_date_overrides`, including the 2026-09-19 override already live.\n\n**Done when:** for each of the twelve Cal.com event types, LSAT U and Cal.com\noffer the same slots for the next 45 days. Prove it with a diff script that pulls\nCal.com `/slots` and LSAT U\'s `slots` action and reports any disagreement. That\nscript is the acceptance test for the whole migration and should be written first.\n\n### Phase 2 — the links Joshua hands out\n\n1. `--personal` mode: a standing, reusable link with its own length and price.\n2. Ten personal rows, one per current Cal.com event type.\n3. `--email` on `booking-link`: draft an email with the link, show it, send only\n   on confirmation.\n\n**Done when:** every student who has a Cal.com link has an LSAT U link that\noffers the same times at the same price, and Joshua can produce and send a new\none in a sentence.\n\n### Phase 3 — lifecycle parity\n\n1. Reminders (offsets per decision 3).\n2. Student reschedule (per decision 4).\n3. Consultation on its own schedule, verified against the live Cal.com page.\n\n**Done when:** a booking made on LSAT U produces the same sequence of emails, and\nthe same options for the student, as one made on Cal.com.\n\n### Phase 4 — cutover\n\n1. Publish the `/book/x/:slug` route (still unpublished as of this writing).\n2. Point the website, the consultation link, and every student at LSAT U.\n3. Hide every Cal.com event type; keep the account open, read-only, until the\n   last already-booked Cal.com session has happened.\n4. Run the stuck-booking sweep one last time; confirm zero future Cal.com\n   bookings; close the account.\n\n**Done when:** no future booking exists on Cal.com and no LSAT U link points at\n`cal.com`.\n\n---\n\n## What must not regress\n\n- **The hold model.** LSAT U\'s expiry-and-sweep is the fix for the Cal.com defect\n  that strands slots. Any change to the claim path keeps it.\n- **Double-booking.** Live Google free/busy is part of the slot answer, not a\n  convenience. A pinned link skips the *policy* rules; it never skips the\n  conflict check.\n- **The 6-business-day window is not the target.** Copying the current LSAT U\n  value forward would reintroduce a problem already solved.\n- **No silent sends.** Every email this work adds is drafted for Joshua and sent\n  on his confirmation.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- Are any of the ten personal event types dead? Several titles suggest former\n  students. Worth pruning before creating ten rows — check each against\n  `students/` before migrating.\n- Cal.com\'s per-event booking window was not readable through the API version\n  used here; the 45-day figure comes from the runbook, not from a live read.\n  Confirm in the dashboard before Phase 1 locks it in.\n\r\nGet-Content: \r\nLine |\r\n   2 |  … IT-SPEC.md; Get-Content -Raw projects/lsatu/supabase/migrations/20260 …\r\n     |                ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\r\n     | Cannot find path \'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\projects\\lsatu\\supabase\\migrations\\20260908120000_one_off_booking_links.sql\' because it does not exist.\r\n'}]

- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\teacher\BookingAccess.tsx` — admin page saves event settings but does not expose the minimum-notice field.

- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\docs\adr\0005-single-session-native-slot-first.md` — accepted decision keeping the Cal.com link live until Joshua retires it.

- Background processes: none.
- Dev servers / ports: none.
- Open worktrees / branches: none started by this task. Root and LSAT U repositories contain substantial pre-existing dirty changes; preserve them.

## Verified state as of 2026-09-14 11:32 Toronto

- LSAT Prep booking form source: required name and email; optional notes and guest emails. It has one event, so no “Choose an Event” step.
- Cal.com event configuration: 70 minutes, 24-hour notice, 45-day rolling window; its configured fields include name, email, optional notes, and guests.
- LSAT Prep’s public booking endpoint returned 18 current slots, earliest Tuesday, September 15 at 1:00 p.m. Toronto time. This was checked earlier today and not re-queried at handoff.
